class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        mem=defaultdict(int)
        def dfs(i,val=0):
            if i==len(s):
                return 1
            if (i,val) in mem
                return mem[i,val]
            p=0
            if s[i]=="D:
                for ind in range(0,val+2): 
                    p+=dfs(i+1,ind)%(10**9+7)
            else:
                for ind in range(val+1,i+3): 
                    p+=dfs(i+1,ind)%(10**9+7)
            mem[i,val]=p
            return p
        return dfs(0)

Let's examine original code:
```
class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        mem=defaultdict(int)
        def dfs(i,val=0):
            if i==len(s):
                return 1
            if (i,val) in mem
                return mem[i,val]
            p=0
            if s[i]=="D:
                for ind in range(0,val+2): 
                    p+=dfs(i+1,ind)%(10**9+7)
            else:
                for ind in range(val+1,i+3): 
                    p+=dfs(i+1,ind)%(10**9+7)
            mem[i,val]=p
            return p
        return dfs(0)
```

Need handle modulo. MOD = 10**9+7. In recursive sum:
```

...

```
Since dfs returns modded, p sum of at most n+1 terms each < MOD, p < ~200*MOD, so p % MOD at end enough. But if n large maybe okay. We can do `p = (p + dfs(...)) % MOD` for safety. But if we mod each addition, fine. Need not use defaultdict. Use dict:
```
mem = {}
...
if (i, val) in mem:
    return mem[(i, val)]
...
mem[(i, val)] = p % MOD
return mem[(i, val)]
```
Need include `from functools import lru_cache`? Could use `@lru_cache(None)` and avoid manual mem. But output fixed code can include imports. Simpler:
```
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        @lru_cache(None)
        def dfs(i: int, val: int) -> int:
            if i == len(s):
                return 1
            if s[i] == 'D':
                total = 0
                for nxt in range(0, val + 1):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            else:
                total = 0
                for nxt in range(val + 1, i + 2):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            return total
        return dfs(0, 0)
```

Need ensure code indentation and syntax:
```
class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        memo = {}

        def dfs(i: int, val: int) -> int:
            if i == len(s):
                return 1
            key = (i, val)
            if key in memo:
                return memo[key]

            total = 0
            if s[i] == 'D':
                for nxt in range(val + 1):
                    total += dfs(i + 1, nxt)
            else:
                for nxt in range(val + 1, i + 2):
                    total += dfs(i + 1, nxt)

            total %= MOD
            memo[key] = total
            return total

        return dfs(0, 0)
```
This is correct. But note `total += dfs(...)` without modulo each addition; total can be up to (n+1)*MOD ~ 2e11, still fine. But Python int arbitrary. At end modulo. However if recursion returns modulo, sum of at most n+1 terms < 201*1e9 = 2e11, okay. But if n larger, still okay. Could do `total = (total + dfs(...)) % MOD` to keep small. Either fine. For performance, modulo each addition okay.

Let's maybe derive iterative DP to compare if our recursive code returns same as known recurrence. Known recurrence:
dp[0][0]=1
for i in range(n):
  if s[i]=='D':
    dp_next[j] = sum_{k=j}^{i} dp[k] for j=0..i+1
  else:
    dp_next[j] = sum_{k=0}^{j-1} dp[k] for j=0..i+1
Answer sum dp[n].

dfs(i,val) = sum over valid next ranks of dfs(i+1,nxt). Base dfs(n,val)=1. Thus dfs(0,0) equals number of rank sequences. This is equivalent to reverse DP. Good.

Let's decide final exact code:

```
class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        memo = {}

        def dfs(i: int, val: int) -> int:
            if i == len(s):
                return 1
            key = (i, val)
            if key in memo:
                return memo[key]

            total = 0
            if s[i] == 'D':
                for nxt in range(val + 1):
                    total += dfs(i + 1, nxt)
            else:
                for nxt in range(val + 1, i + 2):
                    total += dfs(i + 1, nxt)

            total %= MOD
            memo[key] = total
            return total

        return dfs(0, 0)
```

This is clean. But note: In Python, `range(val + 1)` when val is int, okay. `range(val + 1, i + 2)` when val+1 > i+2? But val<=i, so val+1 <= i+1 < i+2, nonempty. Good.

Potential issue: If `s[i] == 'D'`, `for nxt in range(val + 1)` includes nxt=val. We established D requires nxt <= val. Good.

Potential issue: If `s[i] == 'I'`, `for nxt in range(val + 1, i + 2)` includes nxt from val+1 to i+1 inclusive. Good.

```
from collections import defaultdict
class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD=10**9+7
        mem={}
        def dfs(i,val=0):
            if i==len(s):
                return 1
            if (i,val) in mem:
                return mem[(i,val)]
            p=0
            if s[i]=='D':
                for ind in range(0,val+1):
                    p=(p+dfs(i+1,ind))%MOD
            else:
                for ind in range(val+1,i+2):
                    p=(p+dfs(i+1,ind))%MOD
            mem[(i,val)]=p
            return p
        return dfs(0)
```

```
class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        memo = {}

        def dfs(i, val=0):
            if i == len(s):
                return 1
            key = (i, val)
            if key in memo:
                return memo[key]

            if s[i] == 'D':

            return total

        return dfs(0)
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        memo = {}

        def dfs(i, val=0):
            if i == len(s):
                return 1
            key = (i, val)
            if key in memo:
                return memo[key]

            total = 0
            if s[i] == 'D':
                for nxt in range(val + 1):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            else:
                for nxt in range(val + 1, i + 2):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD

            memo[key] = total
            return total

        return dfs(0)
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
class Solution:
    def numPermsDISequence(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        memo = {}

        def dfs(i, val=0):
            if i == len(s):
                return 1
            key = (i, val)
            if key in memo:
                return memo[key]

            total = 0
            if s[i] == 'D':
                for nxt in range(val + 1):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD
            else:
                for nxt in range(val + 1, i + 2):
                    total = (total + dfs(i + 1, nxt)) % MOD

            memo[key] = total
            return total

        return dfs(0)
